;

6.
Semantic Tree-Width for Simple Queries

A
simple regular expression
, or
SRE
, is a regular expression the form
a^{*}
for some letter
a\in\mathbb{A}
or of the form
a_{1}+\dotsb+a_{m}
for some
a_{1},\dotsc,a_{m}\in\mathbb{A}
.

Let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
be the set of all
UCRPQ
whose languages are expressed via
SREs
. Observe that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
is
semantically equivalent
to the class of
UCRPQs
over the closure under concatenation of
simple regular expressions

since
\gamma(x,y)=x\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{e_{1}\cdot e_{2}}}$}\,y
is equivalent to
\gamma^{\prime}(x,y)=x\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{e_{1}}}$}\,z\land z\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{e_{2}}}$}\,y
.
Moreover,
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
also corresponds to
UC2RPQ
whose languages are expressed via
SREs
;
in other words adding two-wayness does not increase the expressivity of the class.

One interest of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
comes from the fact that it is used widely in practice, as recent studies on SPARQL query logs on Wikidata, DBpedia and other sources show that this kind of regular expressions cover a majority of the queries investigated,
e.g.
, 75% of
the “property paths” (
C2RPQ

atoms
) of the corpus of 1.5M queries of Bonifati, Martens and Timm
[
BMT20
, Table 15]
.
An additional interest comes from the fact that the
containment problem
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
is much better behaved than for general
UCRPQs
, since it is in
\Pi^{p}_{2}

[
FGK+20
, Corollary 5.2]
, that is, just one level up the polynomial hierarchy compared to the
CQ

containment problem
, which is in
NP

[
CM77
]
, and in sharp contrast with the costly
ExpSpace
-complete
CRPQ

containment problem

[
CDLV00
,
FLS98
]
.

We devote this section to showing the following result.

\thmSemTwSREpitwo

Observe that
simple regular expressions
are
closed under sublanguages
. Hence, in the light of

Section

1.3
, the
maximal under-approximation
of a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
query by infinitary unions of
CQs
of
tree-width

k
is always equivalent to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
query of
tree-width

k
. We will see how the construction of the
maximal under-approximation
of the previous section can be exploited to improve the complexity from
2ExpSpace
down to
\Pi^{p}_{2}
.

6.1.
Summary Queries

We will first show that the
maximal under-approximation
of
tree-width

k
of a
UC2RPQ
can be expressed as a union of polynomial sized “summary” queries. Each
summary query
represents a union of exponentially-bounded
C2RPQs
sharing some
common structure
.

Summary queries
are normal
UC2RPQ
queries extended with some special kind of atoms, called “
path-
l
approximations
”.
Intuitively, they represent a
maximal under-approximation
of
tree-width

l
of queries of the form
\bigwedge_{i}x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{i}}}$}\,y_{i}
such that
x_{i}\neq y_{j}
for all
i,j
.
Path-
l
approximations may require an exponential size when represented as
UC2RPQs
.
Formally, a
path-
l
approximation
is a query of the form “
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X,Y,\delta)
”
where:

(1)

X
,
Y
, are two disjoint sets of variables of size at most
l
,

(2)

\delta(\bar{z})
is a conjunction of
atoms

\bigwedge_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}A_{i}
where
\bar{z}
contains all variables of
X\cup Y
,

(3)

each
A_{i}
is a
C2RPQ
atom of the form
x\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,y
or
y\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,x
such that
x\in X
,
y\in Y
, and
L_{i}
is a regular language over
\mathbb{A}
.

We give the semantics of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X,Y,\delta)
in terms of infinitary unions of
C2RPQs
.
A query like the one before is defined to be equivalent to the (infinitary) union of all queries
\alpha(\bar{z})\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{P\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{l\!}}}(\delta)}
such that

\alpha
has a
path decomposition
of
width

l
where
X
is the root and
Y
is the leaf,

(5)

that is, the root and leaf
bags
contain precisely the vertices of
X
and
Y
, respectively.
See
Figure

14
for an example.

Figure 14
.

Consider the
path-
l
approximation

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(\{x_{1},x_{2}\},\{y_{1},y_{2}\},\delta)
where
l=2
and
\delta
is depicted on the left. Its semantics contains the
approximation

\alpha(x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2})\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{P\hskip-0.31502ptw}_{l\!}}}(\delta)}
depicted in the middle because it has
path decomposition
of
width

l
verifying (
5
), as shown on the right.

We now simply define a
k
-summary query
as a
C2RPQ
extended with
path-
l
approximation
atoms for any
l\leqslant k
, with the expected semantics.
A
refinement
of a
k
-summary query
is any
C2RPQ
obtained by
replacing
atoms
with
atom refinements
, and
each
path-
l
approximation

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X,Y,\delta)
with any

\alpha(\bar{z})\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{P\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}(\delta)}
verifying (
5
). By definition,
a
database
satisfies a
k
-summary query
if and only if it
satisfies one of its
refinements
.

A
tree decomposition
of a
k
-summary query

\gamma
consists of
a pair
(T,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}})
with
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}}:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}V}(T)\to\mathcal{P}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textit{vars}}(\gamma))

such that:

•

for every classical
atom

x\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,y
in
\gamma
,
there is a
bag

b\in T
such that
\{x,y\}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}}(b)
;

•

for every
path-
l
approximation

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X,Y,\delta)
in
\gamma
,
there are two adjacent
bags

b,b^{\prime}\in T
such that
X\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}}(b)

and
Y\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}}(b^{\prime})
.

The
width
is defined as usual. Then, by

3
, we obtain the following upper bound.

Fact 12
.

For any
k\geqslant 2
, any
refinement
of a
k
-summary query
with
a
tree decomposition
of
width
at most
k
is a
C2RPQ
of
tree-width
at most
k
.

Lastly, a
homomorphism
from a
C2RPQ

\gamma(\bar{z})=\bigwedge_{i}x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{i}}}$}\,y_{i}

to a
summary query

\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})=\bigl(\bigwedge_{j}x^{\prime}_{j}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L^{\prime}_{j}}}$}\,y^{\prime}_{j}\bigr)\wedge\bigl(\bigwedge_{j^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X_{j^{\prime}},Y_{j^{\prime}},\delta_{j^{\prime}})\bigr)

consists of a mapping
f
from variables of
\gamma
to variables of
\delta

such that
f(\bar{z})=\bar{z}^{\prime}
, and for each
i
,
there is an atom
f(x_{i})\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{i}}}$}\,f(y_{i})
in
\delta
.
Note that if there is a homomorphism from
\gamma(\bar{z})
to
\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})
,
then
\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma(\bar{z})
.

Let us fix
\mathcal{L}
to be any class
closed under sublanguages
. For every
\gamma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{C2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
, we define
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
as the set of all
k
-summary queries

\alpha
such that:

(a)

\alpha
has a
fine tagged tree decomposition

(T,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}})
of
width
at most
k
,

(b)

there exists a
strong onto

homomorphism
from a
refinement

\rho
of
\gamma
to
\alpha
,

(c)

T
has at most
2{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}
leaves, and every

non-branching path
of
T
consisting only of
non-atomic

bags
must contain at most two
non-full bags
.

Note that since
\alpha
is a homomorphic image a
refinement
of
\gamma
,
and since
\mathcal{L}
is
closed under sublanguages
, then
\alpha
has only
\mathcal{L}
-labelled
atoms
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
k\geqslant 2
. For every
finite
class
\mathcal{L}

closed under sublanguages
,
and for every
\gamma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{C2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
,
we have:

(1)

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}
,

(2)

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
is a union of polynomial-sized
k
-summary queries
having only

\mathcal{L}
-labelled
atoms
, and

(3)

one can test in
NP
if a
summary query
is part of this union.

Proof 6.2
.

Point (
2
) follows directly from the definition: there are few branches in the decomposition, branches are short, and each
bag
cannot contain more than
(k+1)^{2}\cdot|\mathcal{L}|

atoms
labelled with
\mathcal{L}
-languages.

For point (
3
), recall that one can check if a query has
tree-width
at most
k
in linear time, e.g. using Bodlaender’s algorithm
[
Bod96
, Theorem 1.1]
.

To prove (
1
), notice first that

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}
as a consequence
of
12
.

For the converse
containment
, we use
Corollary

3.6

and prove instead
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
.
Observe that, as corollary of the proof of
Lemma

3.8
, we can assume to have

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
expressed as a union of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{C2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
with a
fine tagged tree decomposition
of
width

k
with at most
2{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}
leaves, and hence it suffices to replace each
non-branching paths
having
non-atomic
bags with
path-
l
approximations
.

Indeed, fix a
fine tagged tree decomposition
and a
trio

f\colon\rho\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}\hskip-8.00003pt\xrightarrow{}$}}}\alpha
. Given a long
non-branching path
from
bag

b_{X}
with
variables
X
to a
bag

b_{Y}
with variables
Y
,
such that
b_{X}
and
b_{Y}
are
non-full
, and no
bag
in between is
atomic
,
define
X^{\prime}\mathrel{\hat{=}}X\setminus Y
and
Y^{\prime}\mathrel{\hat{=}}Y\setminus X
.
Consider the set
\mathcal{S}
of
atoms

u\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,v
of
\gamma
, such that the
path induced

\bigl({b_{i}\choose z_{i}}\bigr)_{i}
by the
refinement
, say

u=w_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{0}}}$}\,w_{1}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{1}}}$}\,\cdots\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{n}}}$}\,w_{n}=v

of
u\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,v
in
\rho

goes through
b_{X}
at some variable of
X^{\prime}
and through
b_{Y}
at some variable
of
Y^{\prime}
, in the sense that
b_{i}=b_{X}
and
z_{i}\in X^{\prime}
for some
i
,
and
b_{j}=b_{Y}
and
z_{j}\in Y^{\prime}
for some
j
.
There exist
i^{\prime},j^{\prime}
such that
f(w_{i^{\prime}})=z_{i}
and
f(w_{j^{\prime}})=z_{j}
,
and w.l.o.g.
i^{\prime}<j^{\prime}
.
Now let
\alpha^{\prime}
be the query obtained from
\alpha
by removing all
atoms
tagged in a bag between
b_{X}
and
b_{Y}
, and add a
path-
l
approximation
query

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime},\delta)

where
\delta
is the conjunction over
u\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,v\in\mathcal{S}
of

w_{i^{\prime}}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}[}L_{i^{\prime}}\cdots L_{j^{\prime}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}]}}}$}\,w_{j^{\prime}}
.
Repeat this operation for every non-trivial
non-branching path
with

non-atomic

bags
. We obtain
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
s.t.

\alpha\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\alpha^{\prime\prime}
, which concludes the proof
that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
.

6.2.
Semantic Tree-Width Problem

With the previous results in place, we now show that the semantic tree-width
k
problem is in
\Pi^{p}_{2}
for
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
, for every
k>1
.

\thmSemTwSREpitwo

*

Proof 6.3
.

It suffices to show the statement for any
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{CRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}~\gamma
. Remember that
\gamma
is of
semantic tree-width

k
if, and only if,
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
. The first ingredient to this proof is the fact that this
containment
has a polynomial counterexample property.

Claim 13
.

If
\gamma\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
then there is a polynomial-sized
expansion

\xi
of
\gamma
such that
\xi\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
.

Proof 6.4
.

Let us call any atom with a language of the form
a^{*}
a
recursive atom
, and any other atom a
non-recursive atom
.
Let
n
be the number of
non-recursive atoms
of
\gamma
. Hence, any refinement
\rho\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{Ref}^{\smash{}}}(\gamma)
has
n
atoms deriving from
non-recursive

atom refinements
, all the remaining ones derive from
recursive

atom refinements
.

We will work with the infinitary union of
conjunctive queries

\mathcal{U}\mathrel{\hat{=}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\cap\textnormal{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}CQ}}
. Note that
\mathcal{U}=\{\alpha\in\textnormal{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}CQ}}\mid\alpha\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.49994ptw}_{k\!}}\text{ and there is }\xi\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{Exp}}(\gamma)\text{ {s.t.} }\xi\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}\hskip-8.00003pt\xrightarrow{}$}}}\alpha\}
.
It is easy to see that
\mathcal{U}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
as a consequence of
3
.
By
Proposition

2.4
, we have
\gamma\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\mathcal{U}
if, and only if, there is some
expansion

\xi
of
\gamma
such that
\xi\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\mathcal{U}
. In turn, this happens if, and only if, there is no
\delta\in\mathcal{U}
such that
\delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi
.

Take any such counterexample
\xi
of minimal size (in number of atoms). We show that for any
internal path
of
\xi
of the shape

\pi=x_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{1}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{1}\dotsb x_{m-1}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{m},

we have
m\leqslant n+1
. Hence, since
\xi
is an
expansion
of a
CRPQ
(
SRE
)
, this means that the size of each
atom expansion
—namely an
expansion
obtained from a query by only expanding one
atom
—of
\xi
is linearly bounded in the size of
\gamma
, and thus that
\xi
is quadratically bounded.

By means of contradiction, if
m>n+1
consider the
expansion

\xi^{\prime}
resulting from “shrinking” the path
\pi
to a path
\pi^{\prime}
of length
n+1
. Hence,
\xi^{\prime}
is smaller than
\xi
, and since
\xi
was assumed to be minimal,
\xi^{\prime}
cannot be a counterexample. Thus, there is some
\delta\in\mathcal{U}
such that
f_{1}:\delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi^{\prime}
for some
homomorphism

f_{1}
. Further, by definition of
\mathcal{U}
, we have
f_{2}:\xi^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}\hskip-8.00003pt\xrightarrow{}$}}}\delta
for some
\xi^{\prime\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{Exp}}(\gamma)
. Consider then the composition
\xi^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}\hskip-8.00003pt\xrightarrow{}$}}}\delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi^{\prime}
of
f_{2}
with
f_{1}
and let us call it
g:\xi^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi^{\prime}
. By definition of
n
there must be at least one atom
x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{i+1}
of the shrunken path
\pi^{\prime}
of
\xi^{\prime}
which either
(i) is not in the image of
f_{1}
, or
(ii) all its
g
-preimages proceed from
atoms

z\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,z^{\prime}
of
\xi^{\prime\prime}
which are in the
expansions
of
recursive atoms
of
\gamma
.
We show that, in both cases, we can replace
x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{i+1}
with a path of
a
’s of any arbitrary length
l>0
, obtaining a
conjunctive query

\xi^{\prime}_{+l}
which
is—still—not a counterexample. In the first case (i), we actually obtain that
\delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi^{\prime}_{+l}
. In the second case (ii), we have to replace each atom
z\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,z^{\prime}
in the
g
-preimage of
x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{i+1}
in
\xi^{\prime\prime}
by an
a
-path of length
l
, obtaining some
expansion

\xi^{\prime\prime}_{+l}
of
\gamma
. We also replace each atom in the
f_{1}
-preimage of
x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{i+1}
by an
a
-path of length
l
obtaining some
\delta_{+l}
such that
\xi^{\prime\prime}_{+l}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}\hskip-8.00003pt\xrightarrow{}$}}}\delta_{+l}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi^{\prime}_{+l}
. Further,
\delta_{+l}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.49994ptw}_{k\!}}
since
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.49994ptw}_{k\!}}
is closed under
refinements
by
3
.
In both cases this shows that
\xi^{\prime}_{+l}
is
not
a counterexample.
In particular, for
l=m-n-1
, we have
\xi^{\prime}_{+l}=\xi
, and this would contradict the fact that
\xi
is a counterexample.
Therefore, there exists a counterexample of polynomial (quadratic) size whenever
\gamma\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
.

The second ingredient is that testing whether a
CQ
is a counterexample is in
co-NP
.

Claim 14
.

The problem of testing, given a
C2RPQ

\gamma
and a
CQ

\xi
, whether
\xi\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
, is in
NP
.

Proof 6.5
.

We first guess a polynomial-sized
k
-summary query

\delta_{\textup{zip}}
and test in
NP
that it is part of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
by
Lemma

6.1
.
Let us call
\Delta
be the equivalent
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textup{UCRPQ}(\textup{SRE})}
query, given by
Lemma

6.1
cum
Lemma

3.8
.
We have to check that there is some
expansion

\delta
of
\Delta
such that there is a
homomorphism

\delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi
.
We first guess a valuation
\mu:{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textit{vars}}(\delta_{\textup{zip}})\to{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textit{vars}}(\xi)
.
Now it remains to check that:

(1)

For every
CRPQ
atom
x\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a^{*}}}$}\,y
of
\delta_{\textup{zip}}
there is an
a
-path in
\xi
from
\mu(x)
to
\mu(y)
.

(2)

Every
path-
l
approximation

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X,Y,\bigwedge_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}A_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))
of
\delta_{\textup{zip}}
contains a
CQ

\delta_{\textup{path}}
admitting a
path decomposition
of
width

l
which starts with the bag
X
and ends with
Y
. And further, there is a homomorphism
h:\delta_{\textup{path}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\xi
which coincides with
\mu
on variables
X\cup Y
.

Observe that these two properties hold true if, and only if, there is some
expansion

\delta
of
\Delta
such that
\delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\gamma
.
It is clear the first point can be achieved in polynomial time (actually, in
NL
) since it is a simple reachability query.
The second point can also be achieved in polynomial time (or in
NL
), since the
fine

path decomposition
of
width

l
can be guessed on-the-fly using
l+1
pointers to the variables of
\gamma
(cf.
Lemma

8.10
). An
NL
algorithm can advance down the
path decomposition
while simultaneously

(1)

guessing the
conjunctive query

\delta_{\textup{path}}
via its
fine

path decomposition
of
width

k
,

(2)

checking that there is a partial
homomorphism
to
\gamma
(i.e., a
homomorphism
from the subquery of
\gamma
restricted to current
bag
’s variables to
\gamma
),

(3)

ensuring that the
CQ

\delta_{\textup{path}}
being built is an element of
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{P}_{\!l}}(X,Y,\bigwedge_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}A_{i}(x_{i},y_{i}))
, which requires to also guess a homomorphism
\rho\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\delta_{\textup{path}}
from a refinement
\rho
of
\bigwedge_{1\leqslant i\leqslant n}A_{i}(x_{i},y_{i})
.

Further, a simple test can ensure that the first and last bags of the decomposition coincide with the guessed assignment
\mu
.
Since the number of pointers (bounded by
k+1
) is fixed, this subroutine is in
NL
, and hence in polynomial time. This yields an
NP
algorithm for testing
\xi\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
.

As a consequence of the two claims, we obtain a
\Sigma^{p}_{2}
algorithm for non-containment of
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
: We first guess an
expansion

\xi
of
\gamma
of polynomial size, and we then test
\xi\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\textup{zip}}}(\gamma)}
in
co-NP
. This gives a
\Pi^{p}_{2}
algorithm for the
semantic tree-width
k
problem
, which is correct by

Lemmas

6.1
and
13
.

7.
Acyclic Queries: the Case
k=1

Observe that in the previous sections we have treated the cases of
semantic tree-width

k
for every
k\geqslant 2
. However, the case
k=1
remains rather elusive so far.
While the
Key Lemma
holds for
k=1
, it proves the computability of an object that
is irrelevant to study
semantic tree-width
1, see
Remark

5.1
.
The problem comes from
Example

3.7
, namely that

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{1\!}}}(\gamma)}\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
.
This is the main obstacle why our approach does not directly yield an algorithm for the case
k=1
, which had been previously solved by Barceló, Romero and Vardi
[
BRV16
]
.
However, as we argue in this section, a rather elegant modification on the notion of
tree-width
allows to use our approach as a unifying framework for both the case
k=1
and the cases

k\geqslant 2
.
Concretely, we introduce a family of classes
\{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.50003ptt\hskip-0.50003ptw}_{k\!}}\}_{k}
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
for every
\gamma
and
k
, and where

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant\textit{poly}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|})}}(\gamma)}
.
As a corollary, we reprove
[
BRV16
, Theorem 6.1]
, namely that the
semantic tree-width
1
problem
is
ExpSpace
-complete. Further, we can also solve the
one-way semantic tree-width
1
problem
, which is outside the scope of
[
BRV16
]
.
Remember that for
k=1
, the
semantic tree-width
and
one-way semantic tree-width
1
problems
are two independent problems, since there are queries of
semantic tree-width
1 but not of
one-way semantic tree-width
1 (
cf.

Remark

3.14
).

7.1.
Contracted Tree-Width

We next formally define the notion of “
contracted tree-width
”, meaning the
tree-width
of the graph obtained by contracting paths (or directed paths) into edges. This altered notion of
tree-width
will allow us to seamlessly prove the case of
k=1
for
Theorem

1.2
.

Given a
C2RPQ

\gamma
, an
internal path
is a sequence of atoms
10
10

10

We write

x\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightleftharpoons{\smash{\lambda}}$}\,y
to mean that there is either an
atom

x\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\lambda}}$}\,y

or
an
atom

x\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xleftarrow{\smash{\lambda}}$}\,y
.

x_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightleftharpoons{\smash{L_{1}}}$}\,x_{1}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightleftharpoons{\smash{L_{2}}}$}\,\cdots\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightleftharpoons{\smash{L_{n-1}}}$}\,x_{n-1}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightleftharpoons{\smash{L_{n}}}$}\,x_{n}

where each
x_{i}
for
i\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\llbracket}1,n-1{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\rrbracket}
has total degree
exactly 2 and is existentially quantified.
Its
contraction
is defined as the edge

x_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{K_{1}\cdot K_{2}\cdots K_{n-1}\cdot K_{n}}}$}\,x_{n},

where
K_{i}\mathrel{\hat{=}}L_{i}
if the
atom
between
x_{i}
and
x_{i+1}
is directed from
left to right, and
K_{i}\mathrel{\hat{=}}L_{i}^{-}
if the
atom
is directed from right to left.
11
11

11

Given a regular language
L
over
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbb{A}^{\pm}}
, we define a regular language
L^{-}
over
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbb{A}^{\pm}}
by induction on regular expressions:
\varnothing^{-}\mathrel{\hat{=}}\varnothing
,
(a)^{-}\mathrel{\hat{=}}a^{-}
,
(a^{-})^{-}\mathrel{\hat{=}}a
,
(L_{1}\cdot L_{2})^{-}\mathrel{\hat{=}}L_{2}^{-}\cdot L_{1}^{-}
,
(L^{*})^{-}\mathrel{\hat{=}}(L^{-})^{*}
and
(L_{1}+L_{2})^{-}\mathrel{\hat{=}}L_{1}^{-}+L_{2}^{-}
. Then, for any graph, there is a path from
x
to
y
labelled by
a word of
L

iff
there is a path from
y
to
x
labelled by a word of
L^{-}
.

Similarly, a
one-way internal path
is a sequence of atoms

x_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{1}}}$}\,x_{1}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{2}}}$}\,\cdots\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{n-1}}}$}\,x_{n-1}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{n}}}$}\,x_{n}

where each
x_{i}
for
i\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\llbracket}1,n-1{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\rrbracket}
has exactly in-degree
and out-degree 1 in
\gamma
and is existentially quantified.
Its
one-way contraction
is defined as the edge

x_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L_{1}\cdot L_{2}\cdots L_{n-1}\cdot L_{n}}}$}\,x_{n}.

A
contraction
(resp.
one-way contraction
) of a
C2RPQ
is any query obtained
by iteratively replacing some
internal paths
(resp.
one-way internal paths
) by
their
contraction
(resp.
one-way contraction
). By definition,
a query is always
equivalent
to any of its
contractions
or
one-way contractions
.

Definition 7.1
.

Define the
contracted tree-width
(resp.
one-way contracted tree-width
)
of a
C2RPQ
as the minimum of the
tree-width
among its
contractions
(resp. of its
one-way contractions
). Let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.50003ptt\hskip-0.50003ptw}_{k\!}}
and
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.80002ptC\hskip-0.50003ptt\hskip-0.50003ptw}_{k\!}}
be, respectively, the set of all
C2RPQs
of
contracted tree-width
at most
k
and of
CRPQs
of
one-way contracted tree-width
at most
k
.

For instance, the query
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma(x_{0},x_{1})\mathrel{\hat{=}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}

has
contracted tree-width
one since the
internal path

x_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,y\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xleftarrow{\smash{M}}$}\,x_{1}

can be
contracted
into
x_{0}\,\raisebox{-0.89996pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{LM^{-}}}$}\,x_{1}
. On the other hand,
its
one-way contracted tree-width
is two, since there is no non-trivial
one-way internal path

as
x_{1}
is an
output variable
.

Note that, by definition:

•

the
contracted tree-width
is at most the
one-way contracted tree-width
, which is
in turn at most the
tree-width
;

•

for
k\geqslant 2
, these notions collapse (by
3
);

•

for
k=1
, both inequalities can be strict.

Moreover, for any
k\geqslant 1
,

contracted tree-width
at most
k
and
one-way contracted tree-width

at most
k
are both closed under
refinements
: if a query has
tree-width
at most
k
, so does any
refinement
thereof. In fact, the
CQs
of
contracted tree-width

1
precisely correspond to what in
[
BRV16
, §5.2.1, p1358]
is known as “pseudoacyclic graph databases”.

Fact 15
.

Let
k\geqslant 1
. For any
CRPQ

\gamma
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
.

Moreover, for any
C2RPQ

\gamma
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
.

Proof 7.2
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}\quad\text{since {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}contractions} preserve semantics,}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\quad\text{by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{obs:equivalence_under_approx_homomorphism}.}

The same arguments work with
one-wayness
.

7.2.
The Key Lemma for Contracted Tree-Width One

We show next that
contracted tree-width

1
has all the needed properties for the analogue of
Key Lemma
for
k=1
to hold.

Lemma 7.3
.

For any
CRPQ

\gamma
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\gamma)}
, where

\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\ell_{1}}=\Theta({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{3}_{\textrm{at}}})
.
Similarly, for a
C2RPQ

\gamma
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\gamma)}
.

Proof 7.4
.

Consider the proof of the
Key Lemma
(
Lemma

3.8
).
We claim that:

(1)

the constructions of

8
and
9

both preserve
contracted tree-width
at most 1 and
one-way contracted tree-width
at most 1;

(2)

the upper bound

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}\in\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{2}_{\textrm{at}}}\cdot 2^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}})

can be easily boiled down to
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}\in\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{3}_{\textrm{at}}})
in the special case of
k=1
.

(
1
) Preservation of
contracted tree-width
.

We claim that all constructions of
Section

5
preserve
contracted tree-width

at most 1. The setting is similar, except that now, a
trio
consists of a triple
f\colon\rho\to\alpha
where
\rho
is a
refinement
of a fixed
C2RPQ

\gamma
, and
\alpha
is a
C2RPQ
of

contracted
tree-width
1. We now apply the constructions not to a decomposition of
\alpha
but to a
fine tagged tree decomposition
of a
contraction
of
\alpha
of
tree-width
1.

Fact 16
.

Let
\gamma
be a
C2RPQ
,
\chi
be a
contraction
of
\gamma
, and

(T,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}})
be a
fine tagged tree decomposition
of
\chi
of
width
at most 1.
Let
z,z^{\prime}\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}}(b)
for some
bag

b\in T
. Then
\gamma\land z\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\lambda}}$}\,z^{\prime}

still has
contracted tree-width
at most 1.

As a consequence, the construction of
8
—which takes us from
Figure

8
to
Figure

10
—preserves
contracted tree-width
1.
Then,
Proposition

4.3
—illustrated in
Figure

9
—can be trivially adapted to our setting as follows:

Fact 17
.

Let
\gamma
,
\gamma^{\prime}
be two
C2RPQ
with a disjoint set of variables.
Let
z
(resp.
z^{\prime}
) be a variable of
\gamma
(resp.
\gamma^{\prime}
).
If both
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
have
contracted tree-width
at most 1, then so does

\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}\land z\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\lambda}}$}\,z^{\prime}
.

As a consequence, the construction of
9
—which takes us from

Figure

10
to
Figure

13
—preserves
contracted tree-width
1.

(
2
) Improved upper bound.
In the proof claiming that in a sufficiently
long
non-branching path
, we can always find two
non-full
,
non-atomic bags
with the same

profile
(see the proof of
9
), we obtain a bound of

\mathcal{O}(2^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}})
. We actually claim that it can be improved to obtain a polynomial
bound. This is because, for
width
1, a
non-full

bag

b
contains exactly 1 variable
z_{b}
.
So, its
profile
consists
simply on a set of
atoms
of
\gamma
—namely the set of
atoms
whose
refinement

induces a
path
which
leaves
the
bag

b
at
z_{b}
.
But we claim that in a
non-branching path
, not all of these
2^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}}

profiles
can occur at the same time. Indeed,
in
tree decompositions
, the set of
bags
containing a given variable must be connected.
This property can be lifted to paths in
tagged tree decompositions
in the following way.

Fact 18
.

Let
(T,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}})
be a
tagged tree decomposition

of some
homomorphism

f\colon\rho\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\alpha
.
Let
\pi
be a path in
\rho
. Assume that:

•

the simple path in
T
from
b
to
b^{\prime\prime}
goes through
b^{\prime}
,

•

there exists some variable
z
of
\alpha
such that
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}[\pi]}

leaves

b
at
z
, and

•

there is no variable like that for the
bag

b^{\prime}
.

Then, there is no variable
z
of
\alpha
such that

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}[\pi]}

leaves

b^{\prime\prime}
at
z
.

Proof 7.5

(Proof of
18
)
.

Fix a
tagged tree decomposition

(T,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}})
of some
homomorphism

f\colon\rho\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{\textit{\tiny hom}}}$}}}\alpha
and
\pi
be a path in
\rho
. Let
b,b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}
be
bags
such that
the simple path in
T
from
b
to
b^{\prime\prime}
goes through
b^{\prime}
.
Say that an
induced path

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}[\pi]}=({b_{i}\choose z_{i}})_{i}

visits
a
bag

b

if
b_{i}=b
for some
i
. Note that this is equivalent to saying that there exists a variable

z
of
\alpha
s.t.
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}[\pi]}

leaves

b
at
z
.
Hence,
18
boils down to the following property:
if
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}[\pi]}

visits
both
b
and
b^{\prime\prime}
, then it must also
visit

b^{\prime}
.
This property holds because by construction, the sequence
(b_{i})_{i}
—namely the projection of

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}[\pi]}
onto
T
—is a path in
T
, with some node repetition.

As a consequence,
if an
atom
occurs in a
bag
, but not in a latter one, then it can never occur again.
Hence, the number of
bags
of size
1
in a
non-branching path
where each
bag

has a different
profile
must be at most
n={\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}
. Hence,
11

yields a bound of
\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{2}_{\textrm{at}}})
.
Finally, we can conclude like in
Section

5.3
: we obtain a tree
with at most
\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}})
leaves, and with
non-branching paths
of length
at most
\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{2}_{\textrm{at}}})
, so the tree has size at most
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}\in\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{3}_{\textrm{at}}})
.
By
local acyclicity
, this concludes the proof of
Lemma

7.3
.
The case of
one-way contracted tree-width
is completely similar.

Lemma 7.6
.

Let
k\geqslant 1
.

(1)

Given a
UCRPQ

\Gamma
, it has
one-way semantic tree-width
at most
1

iff
\Gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\Gamma)}
;

(2)

Given a
UC2RPQ

\Gamma
, it has
semantic tree-width
at most
1

iff
\Gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\Gamma)}
;

where
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}\in\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{3}_{\textrm{at}}})
.

Proof 7.7
.

To prove the first point:

•

if
\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
UCRPQ

\Delta
of
tree-width
at most
1
, then

\Delta\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{T\hskip-1.04996ptw}_{1\!}}}(\Gamma)}
and by
15
and
7.3
,

\Delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\Gamma)}
,
and hence:

\Gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}\Delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\Gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\Gamma.

•

If
\Gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\Gamma)}
,
then
\Gamma
is equivalent to a
UCRPQ
of
contracted tree-width
at
most 1, and hence (by
contraction
) it is equivalent a
UCRPQ
of
tree-width
at most 1.

The second point can be proven similarly.

Corollary 7.8

(Upper bound of
Theorem

1.2
for
k=1
)
.

The
semantic tree-width
1
problem
and
one-way semantic tree-width
1
problem
are in
ExpSpace
.

Proof 7.9
.

The fact that the
semantic tree-width
1
problem
is in
ExpSpace
is actually the main result of
[
BRV16
, Theorem 6.1]
, but we show how the upper bound follows as a direct corollary of
Lemma

7.6
above.
Since
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}\in\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{3}_{\textrm{at}}})
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\gamma)}
is an exponential union of polynomial sized
C2RPQs
, and thus by
Proposition

3.10
the
containment problem

\Gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\Gamma)}
is in
ExpSpace
, and so is the
semantic tree-width
1
problem
(since the converse
containment

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptt\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{1\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell_{1}}}}(\Gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\Gamma
always holds, cf.
Remark

3.2
).
The proof for
one-way semantic tree-width
1
problem
is analogous.

Lastly, note that we can derive from
Lemma

7.3
a characterization of
semantic tree-width
1 somewhat similar to
Section

1.3
.

Corollary 7.10
.

Assume that
\mathcal{L}
is
closed under sublanguages
.

Two-way queries:
For any query
\Gamma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UC2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to an
infinitary union
of
conjunctive queries

of
contracted tree-width
at most
1
;

(2)

\Gamma
has
semantic tree-width
at most
1
;

(3)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UC2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
of
contracted tree-width
at most
1
;

(4)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UC2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
of
tree-width
at most
1
,
where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the closure of
\mathcal{L}
under concatenation and inverses, i.e.

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the smallest class containing
\mathcal{L}
and such that if
K,L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}

then
K\cdot L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
and
K^{-}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

One-way queries:
Similarly, if
\Gamma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UCRPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
, then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to an
infinitary union
of
conjunctive queries

of
one-way contracted tree-width
at most
1
;

(2)

\Gamma
has
one-way semantic tree-width
at most
1
;

(3)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UCRPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
of
one-way contracted tree-width
at most
1
;

(4)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UCRPQ}}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
of
tree-width
at most
1
,
where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the closure of
\mathcal{L}
under concatenation, i.e.

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the smallest class containing
\mathcal{L}
and such that if
K,L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}

then
K\cdot L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

Note in particular how point (4) of each characterization reflects that a
UCRPQ
of

semantic tree-width
1 can have
one-way semantic tree-width
at least 2—as we showed in

Remark

3.14
. More generally, the differences between this
last corollary and
Section

1.3
highlight the different combinatorial behaviour that
semantic tree-width

k
has, depending on whether
k=1
or
k>1
.

Remark 7.11
.

Finally, note that results of
Sections

6
and
7
can be joined in order to show that the
semantic tree-width 1 problems
are decidable in
\Pi^{p}_{2}
for
UC2RPQs
over the closure
under concatenation and inverses of
SREs
(resp. for
UCRPQs
over the closure under concatenation of
SREs
).
12
12

12

While this the whole class of
UCRPQs
over
SREs
has the same expressivity as
UCRPQs
over the closure under concatenation of
SREs
, this is not true if one adds the constraint of having
tree-width
at most 1, see
Corollary

7.10
.

8.
Semantic Path-Width

In this section, we extend our results to
path-width
. Our motivation lies in the fact that
UC2RPQs
of bounded
semantic path-width
admit a
paraNL
13
13

13

This is the parametrized counterpart of non-deterministic logspace.
algorithm for the

evaluation problem
—see
Section

1.3
—to be compared with
FPT
for bounded
semantic tree-width
.

8.1.
Path-Width of Queries

Recall that for tree-width, for any
k\geqslant 2
, we proved that a
CRPQ
is equivalent to a
finite union of
C2RPQs
of
tree-width
at most
k

iff
it is equivalent to finite union of

CRPQs
of
tree-width
at most
k
(
Section

1.3
). In other words,
two-way navigation
does not help to minimize further the
semantic tree-width
of a query that does not use
two-way navigation
. This property does not hold for
k=1

(
Remark

3.14
). We show in
20
that it also does not hold for

path-width
, no matter the value of
k\geqslant 1
.

This motivates the following two definitions:

•

the
semantic path-width
of a
UC2RPQ
is the minimal
path-width

of a
UC2RPQ
equivalent to it

•

the
one-way semantic path-width
of a
UCRPQ
is the minimal
path-width

of a
UCRPQ
equivalent to it.

For a given
UCRPQ
, the two natural numbers are well-defined, and the former is always less or equal to
the letter.
The
semantic path-width
k
problems
ask, given a
UCRPQ
(resp.
UC2RPQ
), if it has

semantic path-width
(resp.
one-way semantic path-width
) at most
k
.

In this section, we first show that the
semantic path-width
k
problems

are decidable (
Section

1.3
), and then
after showing that evaluation of
UC2RPQs
of bounded
path-width
is
NL
(
Lemma

8.10
) we deduce that for the
evaluation problem
for

UC2RPQs
of bounded
semantic path-width
(in particular, this captures the
case of
UCRPQs
of bounded
one-way semantic path-width
) is in

paraNL
when parametrized in the size of the query (
Section

1.3
).

8.2.
Deciding Bounded Semantic Path-Width

The key (implicit) ingredient in the proof of
Sections

1.3
and
1.3
is that
tree-width
at most
k
is closed under
expansions
(
3
). Unfortunately, this property fails for
path-width
.

(a)

Graph
\mathcal{G}_{k}
with a
path decomposition
of
width

k
, with three
bags
.

(b)

Expansion

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{k}
of
\mathcal{G}_{k}
with a
path decomposition
of
width

k+1
, with three
bags
.

(c)

Tree decomposition
of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{k}
of
width

k
, with four
bags
.

(d)

Path decomposition
of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{k}
of
width

k+1
, with three
bags
.

Figure 15
.

The class of multigraphs with
path-width
at most
k\geqslant 1
is not closed under
expansions
:
illustration of a multigraph
\mathcal{G}_{k}
of
path-width

k
(
Figure

15(a)
)
whose
expansion

\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{k}
has
path-width

k+1
(
Figures

15(b)
and
15(d)
)—but

tree-width

k
(
Figure

15(c)
). Set
X
represents a
(k-1)
-clique.

Fact 19
.

For each
k\geqslant 1
, the class of graphs of
path-width
at most
k
is not
closed under
expansions
.

The counterexample is illustrated in
Figure

15
. A formal proof can be found
in
Appendix

C
.

Remark 20
.

Contrary to the case of
semantic tree-width
, for every
k
there are
CRPQs
which are of
semantic path-width

k
but not of
one-way semantic path-width

k
.

Proof 8.1
.

Indeed, let

\displaystyle\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\mathrel{\hat{=}}\hskip 10.00002pt\bigl(

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{1\leqslant i<j\leqslant k-1}x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{a}}$}\,x_{j}\bigr)\;\land\;\bigl(\bigwedge_{1\leqslant i\leqslant k-1}\bigwedge_{0\leqslant j\leqslant 3}x_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{b}}$}\,y_{i}\bigr)

\displaystyle\land\;\bigl(

\displaystyle\bigwedge_{0\leqslant j<3}y_{i}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{c}}$}\,y_{i+1}\bigr)\;\land\;y_{1}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{d}}$}\,z\;\land\;y_{2}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{e}}$}\,z,

whose underlying graph corresponds to
Figure

15(b)
.
Observe that it is a
core
and that only
z
is existentially quantified.
Then in
\gamma_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{y})
, one can replace the two
atoms

y_{1}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{d}}$}\,z\;\land\;y_{2}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{e}}$}\,z
by
y_{1}\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{d\,e^{-}}}$}\,y_{2}
, while preserving the semantics. The underlying graph of this new query being
Figure

15(a)
, it shows that
\gamma_{k}
has
semantic path-width

k
.

Finally, we claim that
\gamma_{k}
has
one-way semantic path-width

k+1
.
The upper bound follows from
Figure

15(d)
.
For the lower bound, consider a
UCRPQ

\Delta_{k}(\bar{x},\bar{y})
such that

\gamma_{k}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}\Delta_{k}
. Since
\gamma_{k}
is a
CQ
, the
equivalence
implies
that there exists an
expansion

\xi
of a
CRPQ
of
\Delta_{k}
such
\gamma_{k}
and

\xi
are homomorphically equivalent. Since
\gamma_{k}
is a
core
, it follows that

\xi
contains it as a subgraph. Hence, the underlying directed multigraph
of the
CRPQ
in
\Delta_{k}
from which
\xi
originated
must contain a
one-way contraction
of
\xi
as a subgraph.
But the only
one-way contraction
of
\xi
is itself, and so it follows that
at least one
CRPQ
in
\Delta_{k}
contains the underlying graph of
\xi
as a subgraph.
Therefore,
\Delta_{k}
has
path-width
at least
k+1
, which concludes the proof
that the
one-way semantic path-width
of
\gamma_{k}
is at least (and hence exactly)
k+1
.

As done for
contracted tree-width
, we define
contracted path-width
.

Definition 8.2
.

Define the
contracted path-width
(resp.
one-way contracted path-width
)
of a
C2RPQ
as the minimum of the
path-width
among its
contractions
(resp. of its
one-way contractions
). Let
\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.50003ptp\hskip-0.50003ptw}_{k\!}}
and
\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.80002ptC\hskip-0.50003ptp\hskip-0.50003ptw}_{k\!}}
be, respectively, the set of all
C2RPQs
of
contracted path-width
at most
k
and of
CRPQs
of
one-way contracted path-width
at most
k
.

The statements and proofs of this section are analogous to the ones of
Section

7
in the context of
contracted tree-width
1. We keep the order and structure to make this correspondence evident.

Again, by definition,
contracted path-width
at most
k
and
one-way contracted path-width

at most
k
are both closed under
refinements
: if a query has width at most
k
, so does any
refinement
thereof.

Fact 21
.

Let
k\geqslant 1
. For any
CRPQ

\gamma
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1P\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
.

Moreover, for any
C2RPQ

\gamma
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{P\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}
.

Proof 8.3
.

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1P\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}\quad\text{since {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}contractions} preserves the semantics,}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\quad\text{by \lx@cref{creftypecap\lx@tilde refnum}{obs:equivalence_under_approx_homomorphism}.}

The same arguments work for
C2RPQs
.

Lemma 8.4
.

For
k\geqslant 1
and
CRPQ

\gamma
, we have

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\gamma)}
, where

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}=\Theta({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{2}_{\textrm{at}}}\cdot({k}+1)^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}})
.
Similarly, for a
C2RPQ

\gamma
,

{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\gamma)}
.

Proof 8.5
.

Consider the proof of the
Key Lemma
(
Lemma

3.8
):
both constructions (
8
and
9
) preserve the
contracted path-width
of
\alpha
if the operations are applied
to a suitable
path decomposition
of a
contraction
of
\alpha
of width
k
.

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
k\geqslant 1
.

(1)

Given
UCRPQ

\Gamma
, it has
one-way semantic path-width
at most
k

iff
\Gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\Gamma)}
;

(2)

Given a
UC2RPQ

\Gamma
, it has
semantic path-width
at most
k

iff
\Gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{C\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\Gamma)}
.

Proof 8.7
.

To prove the first point:

•

if
\gamma
is
equivalent
to a
UCRPQ

\Delta
of
path-width
at most
k
, then

\Delta\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{P\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}(\gamma)}
and by
21
and
8.4
,

\Delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\gamma)}
,
and hence:

\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}\Delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\gamma)}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma.

•

If
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\gamma)}
,
then
\gamma
is equivalent to a
UCRPQ
of
contracted path-width
at
most
k
, and hence it is equivalent a
UCRPQ
of
path-width
at most
k
.

The second point can be proven similarly.

We can now prove the main theorem.

\decidabilitySemPw

Proof 8.8
.

The upper bounds follow from
Lemma

8.6
.
The lower bounds will be shown in
22
. Lastly, to prove the
ExpSpace
upper bound
for
k=1
, we can apply the same trick as in
Corollary

7.8
.

Similarly to
Corollary

7.10
, we can derive from

Lemma

8.4
a characterization of
semantic path-width
at most
k
.

Corollary 8.9
.

Assume that
\mathcal{L}
is
closed under sublanguages
, and let
k\geqslant 1
.

Two-way queries:

For any query
\Gamma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UC2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
, the following are equivalent:

(1)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to an
infinitary union
of
conjunctive queries

of
contracted path-width
at most
k
;

(2)

\Gamma
has
semantic path-width
at most
k
;

(3)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UC2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
of
contracted path-width
at most
k
;

(4)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UC2RPQ}}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
of
path-width
at most
k
,
where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the closure of
\mathcal{L}
under concatenation and inverses, i.e.

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the smallest class containing
\mathcal{L}
and such that if
K,L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}

then
K\cdot L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
and
K^{-}\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

One-way queries:

Similarly, if
\Gamma\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UCRPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
, then the following are equivalent:

(1)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to an
infinitary union
of
conjunctive queries

of
one-way contracted path-width
at most
k
;

(2)

\Gamma
has
one-way semantic path-width
at most
k
;

(3)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UCRPQ}}(\mathcal{L})
of
one-way contracted path-width
at most
k
;

(4)

\Gamma
is
equivalent
to a
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{UCRPQ}}(\mathcal{L}^{\prime})
of
path-width
at most
k
,
where
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the closure of
\mathcal{L}
under concatenation, i.e.

\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
is the smallest class containing
\mathcal{L}
and such that if
K,L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}

then
K\cdot L\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
.

8.3.
Evaluation of Queries of Bounded Semantic Path-Width

In this section, we show that, as a consequence of
Section

1.3
, we can obtain an
efficient algorithm for the
evaluation problem
.

\paraNLEvalBoundedSemPathWidth

The class
paraNL
was introduced in
[
CCDF97
, Definition, p. 123]
under the
name “uniform
NL
+ advice”. It was renamed
paraNL
in

[
FG03
, Definition 1, p. 294]
. For the sake of simplicity,
instead of either of those definitions, we use the characterization of
paraNL

proven in
[
FG03
, Theorem 4, p. 296]
.

We define
paraNL
as the class of parametrized languages
L\subseteq\Sigma^{*}\times\mathbb{N}

for which there is a Turing machine
M

s.t.

M\text{ accepts }\langle{\color[rgb]{0.9219,0.2305,0.3516}x},\,{\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4023,0.8398}k}\rangle\quad\text{{iff}}\quad\langle
x
,\,
k
\rangle\in L,
input
parameter

and, moreover,
M
runs in non-deterministic space

f({\color[rgb]{0.2188,0.4023,0.8398}k})+\mathcal{O}(\log(|{\color[rgb]{0.9219,0.2305,0.3516}x}|))
,
where
f\colon\mathbb{N}\to\mathbb{N}
is a computable function.
A typical example of
paraNL
problem is the model-checking problem for first-order logic on
finite structures, when parametrized by the maximum degree of the structure

[
FG03
, Example 6]
.

To show
Section

1.3
, we first focus on the evaluation of queries of bounded
path-width
.

Lemma 8.10
.

For each
k\geqslant 1
, the
evaluation problem
, restricted to
UC2RPQs
of

path-width
at most
k
, is
NL
-complete.

(a)

Partial homomorphism computed at the fourth step of the algorithm.

(b)

Partial homomorphism computed at the fifth step of the algorithm.

Figure 16
.

Algorithm to evaluate
C2RPQs
of
path-width
at most
k
(here
k=2
)
in non-deterministic logspace. Each subfigure represents: (i) on the left-hand side, a

path decomposition
of a
C2RPQ
, (ii) on the right-hand side, a
graph database
,
and (iii) a partial
homomorphism
from the
C2RPQ
to the
database
.

Proof 8.11
.

Lower bound.

NL
-hardness direected follows from the
NL
-hardness of
the reachability problem in directed graphs, see e.g.
[
AB09
, Theorem 4.18, p. 89]
.

Upper bound, first part: with the
path decomposition
.

First, we assume that a
tagged path decomposition
of width at most
k
of the query of
is also provided as part of the input. Moreover, we assume w.l.o.g. that the input is
C2RPQ
—the extension to
UC2RPQ
being straightforward. So, we are given as input:

•

a database together with a tuple of nodes
G(\bar{u})
,

•

a
C2RPQ

\gamma(\bar{x})
, and

•

a
tagged path decomposition

\langle T,{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{v}},{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathbf{t}}\rangle
of
width
at most
k
of
\gamma(\bar{x})
.

The algorithm, illustrated in
Figure

16
, maintains a partial homomorphism

f\colon X\rightharpoonup G

of these variables onto
G
.
We scan the
bags
of the decomposition from left to right.

•

Initially—before even scanning the first
bag
—
f
is the map with empty domain.

•

Then, when scanning the
i
-th bag
b_{i}
,
we start by restricting
f
to variables of
\mathop{\textrm{dom}}(f)\cap b_{i}
.
Then, we extend
f
so that it is defined on the whole
bag

b_{i}
.
For every variable
y
in
b_{i}\smallsetminus\mathop{\textrm{dom}}(f)
:

–

if it belongs to
\bar{x}
, say
y=x_{i}
,
we let
f(x_{i})\mathrel{\hat{=}}u_{i}
;

–

otherwise, we non-deterministically guess the value of
f(y)
.

We then check, for every
atom

x\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,y
of
\gamma(\bar{x})
which is
tagged
in
the current
bag

b_{i}
if there is a path from
f(x)
to
f(y)
labelled by a word of
L
in
G
. If not, we reject.

If the algorithm manages to scan the whole bag decomposition without rejecting, it accepts.

Completeness of the algorithm is trivial. Correctness follows from the fact that if
a variable occurs in
bags

b_{i}
and
b_{k}
with
i\leqslant k
, then it must also belong to every

bag

b_{j}
for
j\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\llbracket}i,k{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\rrbracket}
. As a consequence, a variable
x
is assigned
exactly one value
f(x)
during the whole process.

Concerning the space complexity:

•

By construction, at the
i
-th step of the algorithm,
f
is defined exactly on
b_{i}
, so on at most
k+1
variables.
So,
f
can be stored in space
(k+1)\log(|G|)
;

•

we need a counter with
\log(|T|)
bits to scan through the
tagged path decomposition
,

•

each atomic check—checking if there is an
L
-labelled path from
f(x)
to
f(y)
—can be done in non-deterministic space
c\cdot(\log(|G|)+\log(|\mathcal{A}_{L}|))
,
where
\mathcal{A}_{L}
is an NFA for
L
, using a straightforward adaptation of the
NL
algorithm for graph connectivity; note that these atomic checks are
independent of one another, so we can reuse this space.

Overall, the algorithm runs in non-deterministic space

\mathcal{O}(k\log(|G|)+\log(|T|))=\mathcal{O}(\log(|G|)+\log(|T|))
,
which is logarithmic in the size of the input.

Upper bound, second part: without the
path decomposition
.

Then, we claim that the original problem—when the
tagged tree decomposition
is not part of
the input—also lies in
NL
. This is because one can compute, from
\gamma
, a

path decomposition
in (deterministic) logarithmic space by
14
14

14

This result is an
adaption of a similar statement for
tree-width

[
EJT10
, Theorem I.1, p. 143]
. Note that the promise that the query has bounded
path-width
—in fact bounded
tree-width
suffices—in a crucial assumption of
[
EJT10
, Theorem I.1, p. 143]
.

[
KM10
, Theorem 1.3, p. 2]
. Then, a
path decomposition
can be turned into a

tagged path decomposition
in (deterministic) logarithmic space by tagging an
atom

x\,\raisebox{-0.91995pt}{$\xrightarrow{\smash{L}}$}\,y
in the first bag containing both
x
and
y
.
The conclusion follows since functions computable in non-deterministic logarithmic space
are closed under composition
[
AB09
, Lemma 4.17, p. 88]
.

We can now conclude with
Section

1.3
, namely that the
evaluation problem
for
UC2RPQs
of

semantic path-width

k
is in
paraNL
.

Proof 8.12

(Proof of
Section

1.3
.)
.

Given a
UC2RPQ

\Gamma(\bar{x})
of
semantic path-width
at most
k
and a database
G(\bar{u})
, we
first compute
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\Gamma)}
—where
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}=\Theta({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{2}_{\textrm{at}}}\cdot({k}+1)^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}})
—, which is
equivalent
to

\Gamma
by
Lemma

8.6
.
Then, we use
Lemma

8.10
to evaluate each

\delta(\bar{x})\in{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\textnormal{App}_{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\mathcal{1\hskip-0.56001ptC\hskip-0.35002ptp\hskip-0.35002ptw}_{k\!}}}^{\smash{\star,\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}}}(\Gamma)}
on
G(\bar{u})
.
If one of the queries accepts, we accept. Otherwise, we reject.

The non-deterministic space needed by the algorithm is:

•

\mathcal{O}({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell})
bits to enumerate and store
\delta(\bar{x})
, where
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}=\Theta({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|^{2}_{\textrm{at}}}\cdot({k}+1)^{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|_{\textrm{at}}}})

•

\mathcal{O}(\log{|G|}+\log{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\delta{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}})\subseteq\mathcal{O}(\log{|G|}+\log{|{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}|}+\log{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}})
to evaluate

\delta(\bar{x})
on
G(\bar{u})
, by
Lemma

8.10

and since
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\delta{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\leqslant{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\cdot{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\ell}
.

Overall, we use non-deterministic space
f({\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|}\Gamma{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\|})+\mathcal{O}(\log(|G|))

where
f
is a single exponential, which concludes the proof.

9.
Lower Bounds for Deciding Semantic Tree-Width and Path-Width

An
ExpSpace
lower bound follows by a straightforward adaptation from the

ExpSpace
lower bound for the case
k=1

[
BRV16
, Proposition 6.2]
.

Lemma 22

(Lower bound of
Theorem

1.2
)
.

For every
k\geqslant 1
, the following problems are
ExpSpace
-hard, even if restricted to
Boolean CRPQs
:

•

the
semantic tree-width
k
problem
;

•

the
one-way semantic tree-width
k
problem
;

•

the
semantic path-width
k
problem
;

•

the
one-way semantic path-width
k
problem
.

We say that a
C2RPQ
is
connected
when its underlying undirected graph is connected.
We first give a small useful fact.

Fact 23

(Implicit in
[
BRV16
, Proof of Proposition 6.2]
)
.

(1)

Let
G,G^{\prime}
be two
databases
and
\delta
be a
connected

Boolean

C2RPQ
.
If the disjoint union
G\mathrel{\immediate\immediate\immediate{\color[rgb]{0.3438,0.0195,0.0273}+}}G^{\prime}
(i.e., the union assuming
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}V}(G)
and
{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}V}(G^{\prime})
are disjoint)
satisfies

\delta
, then either
G

satisfies

\delta
or

G^{\prime}

satisfies

\delta
.

(2)

Let
\gamma,\delta
be
Boolean

C2RPQs
. If
\delta
is
connected

and
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\delta
, then there exists a
subquery

\gamma^{\prime}
of
\gamma
,
obtained as connected component of
\gamma
, such that

\gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\delta
.

Notice first that if
\gamma
and
\delta
are
CQs
then the proof of

23
follows directly from the equivalence of
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\delta
(resp.
G

satisfies

\gamma
) and the existence of a
homomorphism
from
\delta
to
\gamma
(resp.
\gamma
to
G
).

Proof 9.1
.

We first prove the second point.
Write
\gamma\mathrel{\hat{=}}\gamma_{1}\land\ldots\land\gamma_{n}
where
\gamma_{1},\ldots,\gamma_{n}

are connected components of
\gamma
, and assume by contradiction that
for all
i
,
\gamma_{i}\not\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\delta
. Then there exists a database
G_{i}

such that
G_{i}

satisfies

\gamma_{i}
but not
\delta
.
Consider the disjoint union
G=G_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}+}}\ldots\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}+}}G_{n}
.

On the one hand, since the
\gamma_{i}
’s have disjoint variables
and
G_{i}

satisfies

\gamma_{i}
for each
i
, then
G

satisfies

\gamma
.
On the other hand,
G
cannot
satisfy

\delta
:
if there was a
homomorphism
from
\delta
to
G
,
since
\delta
is connected, there would exist an index
i

such that
\delta
is mapped on
G_{i}
, which would contradict the
fact that
G_{i}
does not
satisfy

\delta
.
Hence,
G
does not
satisfy

\delta
, which contradicts the containment

\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\delta
.

To prove the first point, we simply apply the second one, by letting
\gamma

be the conjunction of the canonical
CQ
associated with
G
and
G^{\prime}
—which
is in fact the canonical
CQ
associated with
G\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}+}}G^{\prime}
. From the assumption
that
G\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}+}}G^{\prime}

satisfies

\delta
it follows that
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\delta

and so, by the first point, there is either a homomorphism from
\delta

to
G
or from
\delta
to
G^{\prime}
.

We can then prove
22
.

Proof 9.2

(Proof of
22
)
.

Fix
k\geqslant 1
. We focus on
semantic tree-width
, but the exact same reduction works for the other three problems.
We introduce an intermediate problem, called
the
asymmetric containment problem for tree-width
k
:
given two
Boolean

CRPQs

\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
,
where
\gamma
has
tree-width

k
,
\gamma^{\prime}
is
connected

and does
not
have
semantic tree-width

k
,
it asks whether
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
.
The proof of the lemma then contains two parts:

(1)

first, we reduce the
asymmetric containment problem for tree-width
k

to the
semantic tree-width
k
problem
,

(2)

then, we prove that the
asymmetric containment problem for tree-width
k
is

ExpSpace
-hard.

(1):
We reduce the instance
(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})

of the
asymmetric containment problem for tree-width
k

to the instance
\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}
of the
semantic tree-width
k
problem
. We simply have
to check that
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
if and only if

\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}
has
semantic tree-width

k
. The left-to-right
implication is straightforward since
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}

implies that
\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}\gamma
and
\gamma
was assumed to
have
tree-width

k
.
For the converse implication, if
\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}\delta

where
\delta
is a
UC2RPQ
of
tree-width

k
then write

\delta=\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i}
where the
\delta_{i}
’s are
C2RPQs

and let
\delta_{i,1},\ldots,\delta_{i,k_{i}}
be the connected components
of
\delta_{i}
.

Since for each
i
we have

\delta_{i}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\delta\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\equiv}}\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
, by

23
, there exists
j_{i}
such that

\delta_{i,j_{i}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
.
Let
\delta^{\prime}\mathrel{\hat{=}}\bigvee_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{i,j_{i}}
so that, by construction

\delta^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
. However, note that
\delta^{\prime}
has
tree-width
at most
k

but
\gamma^{\prime}
was assumed not to have
semantic tree-width

k
,
hence
\delta^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subsetneqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
,
so there exists
G^{\prime}
such that:

\immediate\immediate G^{\prime}\text{ {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}satisfies} }\gamma^{\prime}\quad\text{ and }\quad G^{\prime}\text{ does not {\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}satisfy} }\delta^{\prime}.

(6)

We now prove that
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
. Let
G
be a

database

satisfying

\gamma
. Then the disjoint union
G\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}+}}G^{\prime}

satisfies

\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}

since
G

satisfies

\gamma
,
G^{\prime}

satisfies

\gamma^{\prime}
and
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}

are Boolean so we can assume w.l.o.g. that they have disjoint variables.
As a consequence,
G\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}+}}G^{\prime}

satisfies

\delta
and hence
\delta^{\prime}
,
so there exists
i
such that
G\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}+}}G^{\prime}

satisfies

\delta_{i,j_{i}}
.
Since
\delta_{i,j_{i}}
is
connected
, either
G

satisfies

\delta_{i,j_{i}}
or
G^{\prime}

satisfies

\delta_{i,j_{i}}
.
By
Equation

6
, the latter cannot hold,
so
G

satisfies

\delta_{i,j_{i}}
and hence
\gamma^{\prime}
.

Therefore, we have shown that for each
database

G
that
satisfies

\gamma
, then
G

satisfies

\gamma^{\prime}
, i.e.,
\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
. Overall,

\gamma\land\gamma^{\prime}
has
semantic tree-width

k
if and only if

\gamma\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}\gamma^{\prime}
.

(2):

We now show that the
asymmetric containment problem for tree-width
k
is

ExpSpace
-hard.
It was shown in
[
Fig20
, Lemma 8]
that
the
containment
of

was still
ExpSpace
-hard
when restricted to inputs of the form:
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma()=\footnotesize\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\footnotesize\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0.0195,0.2109,0.2539}\subseteqq}}^{?}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\footnotesize\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\footnotesize\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=\delta(),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{1}}
⋮
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L_{p}}

where
